## Tessera-specific Memorial state lineage (this overnight cycle)

The 2026-05-15 → 2026-05-16 scoping cycle produced **two violations** of the MD-F6 sub-variant within hours of each other:

| # | Event | Memorial D state |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Pre-cycle (inherited DeploySignal post-Phase-3.d.D close 2026-05-07) | 20V/8C; 8th CONFIRMATION class at 4 sub-instances (Q60 V1 LS-1 + Q60 LS-2 + Q64 Phase 4 + Q66 SLICE 1 LS-1) |
| 1 | v0.1 SCOPING-MEMO emit → Reviewer F1 (missed Addition #25/#26 existing primitives) → v0.2 amendment | **21V/8C** (5th sub-instance: file-opened-discipline-paired-with-candidate-set-enumeration at SCOPE-PROPOSAL fidelity; MD-F6 sub-variant memorialized) |
| 2 | Q-R01-SPEC v0.1 emit → Reviewer F1 (missed actual `config.ts` inline-union state at SHA `5a72371`) → v0.2 amendment | **22V/8C** (6th sub-instance: MD-F6 at SPEC fidelity; SECOND occurrence same session; demonstrates discipline-application-gap pattern is stickier than memorialization) |
| 3 | Phase 1 close walk (R15) at 2026-05-17; R02-R14 produced 39 methodology-class violations + 0 methodology-class confirmations (aggregate) + 1 Memorial-D class violation (R02 ARCHITECT pre-emit-grilling, explicitly Sub-instance MD-F6; see MEMORIAL.md line 215) + 0 Memorial-D class confirmations | **23V / 8C** |

**Critical discipline-archive observation:** memorializing MD-F6 at sub-instance #5 (v0.2 SCOPING-MEMO) did NOT prevent sub-instance #6 (Q-R01-SPEC v0.1) within hours. **Structural fix landed at anchor PR #35**: mandatory `## Existing architectural surface (REVIEWER-ANCHOR)` section in `templates/Q-NN-SPEC-TEMPLATE.md` + `integrations/superpowers-claude-code/scripts/verify-citations.sh` mechanical verification. Converts the file-opened discipline from declarative ("did you open the file?") → structural (table must exist with grep-evidenced citations) + mechanical (script must produce 0 failures on resolution).

Post-anchor-PR-35-merge, Tessera SPEC-fidelity drafts (Q-R02-SPEC.md onward) MUST include the section. v0.3 SCOPING-MEMO + Q-R01-SPEC retroactively applied the section at SCOPE-PROPOSAL + SPEC fidelities respectively (commits `e8de97f` + `dee126d`). Both would have caught the originating MD-F6 violations at draft time if the section + script had been in place.

## Phase shard index (R42 sharding 2026-05-19)

Past-phase MEMORIAL entries live in phase-scoped shards. New round entries append to this active file under per-round headers. At each phase close, active-file content rolls to a new `MEMORIAL-PHASE-N.md` shard; the active file resets to header + index + open-phase entries.

| Shard | Round range | Path | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Phase 1 + calibration | R01–R19 | [`MEMORIAL-PHASE-1.md`](MEMORIAL-PHASE-1.md) | CLOSED (Phase 1 close at R15; calibration R16–R19) |
| Phase 2 + post-Phase-2 hygiene | R20–R41 | [`MEMORIAL-PHASE-2.md`](MEMORIAL-PHASE-2.md) | CLOSED (Phase 2 close at R37 WAVE-GATE-05; safe-continuation R38–R41) |
| Active | R42+ | (this file) | OPEN |

**Read protocol (effective R42):**

- **Default per-round read:** Architect / Reviewer / Memorial-Updater read this active file in full.
- **Cross-phase reference:** read the relevant phase shard on demand (e.g., to locate the canonical violation that derived a specific REINFORCED line; to verify a prior round's Memorial-D state cell; to resolve a `MEMORIAL.md:NNNN` line-number back-reference).
- **Memorial-Updater writes** new entries below the round header in this active file. Append behavior unchanged.
- **At next phase close:** active content rolls to a new `MEMORIAL-PHASE-N.md`; index gains one row; active file resets to header + index + new-phase rounds.
- **Original `MEMORIAL.md:NNNN` back-references inside shards:** refer to line numbers in pre-R42 MEMORIAL.md. `cat MEMORIAL-PHASE-1.md MEMORIAL-PHASE-2.md` (stripping the 12-line per-shard headers) reproduces the pre-shard ordering for line-number resolution.

## R79 — ARCHITECT (full-tier) (2026-05-20)

VIOLATION: pre-emit-grilling | forward-protection-audit-incomplete | Architect grilling at § 1.4 predicted AC-R78-14 would flip because R79 modifies files in R78's frozen-path list. However, R77's frozen-path list ALSO includes `tools/build-canned-demos.ts`, which R79 was authorized to modify. Architect did not exhaustively audit ALL prior forward-protection ACs (R77-14, R76-14, etc.) against R79's ALLOWED_SET. Result: only AC-R78-14 was predicted to flip; AC-R77-14 also flipped at chore-A (empirically fail=8, not 7). Architect added a note that the gap was "known" (§ 1.4-note), but the gap is the violation — the spec should have explicitly audited the full forward-protection surface or HALTed with "multiple flip candidates possible; requires operator authorization". The pattern: AC-R77-14 and AC-R78-14 have identical structure (round-start test freezes files that next round was authorized to modify), yet only one was predicted. | R79 | ARCHITECT

VIOLATION: halt-discipline (implicit sub-pattern) | forward-protection-flip-acknowledgment-insufficiency | Architect § 1.4-note acknowledged "AC-R78-14 was predicted; AC-R77-14 was an Implementer-discovered surprise" and framed the gap as acceptable ("empirical ground truth discovered at chore-A"). Per CLAUDE-COMMON.md REINFORCED 2026-05-18 encode-actual-results-verbatim discipline, any deviation of predicted vs. observed counts warrants a halt-condition gate. The spec prescribed a halt-condition ("if fail > 7: investigate...HALT") but framed the discovered 8th flip as a known-unknown acceptable at chore-A, downgrading it from HALT-worthy to note-worthy. This is a soft-closure of a halt-condition rather than an explicit HALT. The methodology distinction matters: if a halt-condition fires, the procedure should escalate; "acknowledging it in footnotes" is not a substitute for structured escalation. | R79 | ARCHITECT

CONFIRMATION: grilling-completeness-gates-applied | Architect pre-emit grilling covered: Superpowers brainstorm (3 approaches with explicit rejection rationale); design phase (component boundaries, layout architecture, schema additions); mechanism + load-bearing decisions; per-file pseudocode; AC table (14 ACs); cross-section consistency walk (15 named entities verified); spec-internal contradiction sweep; empirical-premise verification; EMPIRICAL.sh probe-run. No violations discovered at spec-emit time except the forward-protection-flip-completeness gap (above). | R79 | ARCHITECT

CONFIRMATION: cross-project-rules-applied-upfront | All 7 cross-project rules active per Q-R79-SPEC § 10. Rule 1 (empirical-command-attestation): Q-R79-EMPIRICAL.sh uses --test-reporter=tap per R77 lesson. Rule 7 (Rule 7 surface propagation): the forward-protection-flip-completeness gap reflects the gap between "Architect documents the AC-R78-14 prediction" and "Architect exhaustively audits all forward-protection ACs at once" — structural discipline vs. checklist discipline. Surface (a) SPEC-AUTHORING-CHECKLIST.md § "Rule 7 self-application gate" should have caught this; it did not explicitly mandate "enumerate all forward-protection-ACs in prior rounds and check each against round-start-to-chore-A diff". Rule 7 self-application gate is currently advisory, not mandatory at spec-emit. | R79 | ARCHITECT

## R79 — IMPLEMENTER (full-tier) (2026-05-20)

VIOLATION: halt-discipline | spec-not-amended-post-disposition (MAJOR-1) | When EMPIRICAL.sh Block 3 produced TAP `# fail` = 8 (chore-A expected 7), halt-condition 3 mandated: "If `# fail` > 7: investigate (R79 broke something else; HALT)". Implementer did write DIAGNOSTIC-R79-AC-R77-14-forward-protection-flip.md (halt fired correctly). Then, instead of setting STATUS: ESCALATE in NEXT-ROLE.md and waiting for operator disposition, Implementer self-amended Q-R79-EMPIRICAL.sh Block 3: changed EXPECTED_FAIL from 7 → 8 with explanatory comment, then routed to REVIEWER with STATUS: MERGE-READY. This is the "spec-not-amended-post-disposition" pattern from R25 MAJOR-2/MAJOR-3: empirical-attestation-vs-prediction gap resolved by Implementer-direct modification of spec triad rather than HALT → ESCALATE → Architect amends → resume. Correct procedure: write DIAGNOSTIC (done), escalate (not done), let Architect amend spec (did not happen), resume with amended script (did not happen). | R79 | IMPLEMENTER

VIOLATION: prefix-continuity-invariant (CLAUDE-COMMON.md) | spec-triad-amendment-unilateral | Q-R79-EMPIRICAL.sh is part of the spec triad (committed by Architect at 761fc06). Per CLAUDE-COMMON.md § "Within-round prefix-continuity invariant": "once the Architect commits the spec triad, no role may modify the contents of Q-${round}-SPEC.md, Q-${round}-SPEC-AUDIT.md, Q-${round}-EMPIRICAL.sh (beyond pre-prescribed placeholder substitutions such as SHA injection blocks)." The EXPECTED_FAIL change is not a placeholder substitution. The amendment was unilateral (Implementer alone) rather than architect-mediated (operator-authorized Architect amendment). This is the first Implementer-direct spec-triad-amendment at Tessera; the pattern previously appeared at the Architect role (R25 MAJOR-2/MAJOR-3) and now extends to Implementer side. | R79 | IMPLEMENTER

VIOLATION: encode-actual-results-verbatim (CLAUDE-COMMON.md REINFORCED 2026-05-18) | attestation-verbatim-scope-narrowed | Implementer's role per encode-actual-results-verbatim is to record OBSERVED values verbatim in NEXT-ROLE.md attestations (e.g., "TAP pass=582, fail=8, tests=594"), not to amend the binding-command harness so the observed values match the expected values. The Implementer correctly observed fail=8 but then narrowed the attestation-scope by modifying EXPECTED_FAIL to 8 rather than attesting "observed fail=8 ≠ spec-predicted fail=7 — AC-R77-14 also flipped". The discipline assumes the Implementer is the passive observer (records what happened); instead, Implementer became an active editor (changed the pass-condition to match the observation). | R79 | IMPLEMENTER

Implementation faithfully follows spec; spec amendment recommended for a future round (rename field or amend JSDoc on DsToTesseraEventResponse.freeze_hook_activated to read "deploy-event accepted and forwarded to any wired subscriber"; subscriber-count agnostic). | R66 | ARCHITECT

VIOLATION: declared-but-unenforced-event-emitter-typing | engine/ds-integration/event-consumer.ts:52-55 exports `DsEventConsumerEvents` interface (`activate: [DeployEventPayload]; parse_error: [string]`) but the class `DsEventConsumer extends EventEmitter` (line 169) does not use it as a generic parameter. Node `EventEmitter` accepts any string event name with any args, so a typo at emit (`this.emit('activte', ...)`) would compile silently. The interface is documentation-grade, not a compile-time gate. MINOR-2 in REVIEWER-REPORT-R66; resolution either (a) use typed `EventEmitter<DsEventConsumerEvents>` per Node ≥ 23 typings or (b) drop the interface to avoid implying a guarantee it does not provide. | R66 | IMPLEMENTER

VIOLATION: factory-state-cleanup-gap | engine/ds-integration/freeze-hook-factory.ts:110-113 (timer callback) and :125-131 (`cancelActivation`) set `state.active = false` but never clear `state.cluster_event_id` or `state.until_ts`. After deactivation, `getState()` returns `{ active: false, cluster_event_id: 'evt-N', until_ts: T }` — stale identifiers. Per engine/events/freeze-hook.ts:24-28 these fields are informational-only at the freeze decision, so not a freeze-correctness bug; but any observability hook inspecting `getState()` post-deactivation would receive stale data. No AC verifies state-hygiene. MINOR-3 in REVIEWER-REPORT-R66; recommended fix: `delete state.cluster_event_id; delete state.until_ts` in both deactivation paths, OR explicit JSDoc that these fields are sticky-after-deactivation. | R66 | IMPLEMENTER

VIOLATION: test-import-ordering-convention | test/q66-ds-integration-event-consumer.test.ts:332-333 places `import type { PerShardResidual }` and `import type { ExtendedSampleObservation }` AFTER the `describe(...)` call at line 78-322 and the standalone `freshResidual` / `freshObs` fixture functions. ES modules hoist imports so runtime is correct, but the layout is contrary to codebase convention (sibling files like test/q65-ds-integration-feed.test.ts group imports at top) and obscures the module's dependency graph for a cold reader. MINOR-4 in REVIEWER-REPORT-R66; resolution: move to the import block at lines 4-22. | R66 | IMPLEMENTER

For multi-round attestation-archeology purposes, the dual-value line is mildly confusing. MINOR-5 in REVIEWER-REPORT-R66; full-disclosure pattern is correct (spec-deviance-disclosure honored), but a follow-up cleanup might render this as a single value with a footnote referencing the resolution chain. | R66 | ARCHITECT

VIOLATION: ac-r70-3-regex-weakly-discriminating | Q-R70-SPEC.md § 4.2 line 808 prescribes the test assertion `assert.match(r.output, /shard-00.*shard-01.*shard-02/);` for AC-R70-3 (common-mode-rack). The Implementer faithfully copied at test/q70-demo-scenario.test.ts:43. The regex matches not only the candidate-listing line `  • rack rack-A — 3 members: shard-00, shard-01, shard-02` (the intended discriminator) but also the topology header `  rack-A:  shard-00  shard-01  shard-02` (static; unrelated to candidate output). The regex would PASS even if the candidate-listing line were elided, as long as the topology header rendered. Combined with structured-field assertions (`common_mode_candidates === 1`, `firing_shards.sort() === [shard-00..02]`), the test is overall discriminating, but the regex itself is redundantly weak. Latent regression risk for future renderer refactors. REVIEWER-REPORT-R70.md MINOR-4. | R70 | ARCHITECT

VIOLATION: dashboard-reasoning-text-contradicts-candidate-count | topology-spanning-common-mode scenario's reasoning panel + description field claim "BFS reaches the cooling_zone node common to both racks and surfaces ONE cooling-zone-level candidate" but actual terminal_state.common_mode_candidates has THREE entries: rack-A (member_count=2), rack-B (member_count=2), cz-1 (cooling_zone, member_count=4). With `candidate_node_kinds: ['cooling_zone', 'rack', 'psu']` per Q-R71-SPEC.md § 2.1 + § 4.1 scenario-8, the engine emits all candidate-eligible nodes that meet min_member_count — it cannot surface "ONE" candidate without filtering kinds. The Architect prescribed BOTH the reasoning text ("ONE cooling-zone-level candidate") AND the topology configuration (kinds = cooling_zone + rack + psu) that contradict each other. Source: coordination/specs/Q-R71-SPEC.md § 4.1 scenario-8 pseudocode + § 4.2 topology-spanning-common-mode reasoning row. Severity MAJOR per REVIEWER-REPORT-R71.md MAJOR-2. | R71 | ARCHITECT

VIOLATION: spec-AC-coverage-gap-for-pedagogical-properties | Spec AC-R71-9 binds `fleet_fired === true` (weak — satisfied by ANY drift large enough to cross threshold) instead of "fleet fires before any per-shard fires" (the pedagogical claim). Spec AC-R71-11 binds "cooling_zone candidate with member_count ≥ 4" instead of "exactly one candidate" or "cooling_zone is the only candidate kind." Root cause for MAJOR-1 + MAJOR-2 above. Spec § 9 commitment audit (per R65 reinforcement) claimed every behavioral commitment is bound by an AC, but the `reasoning`-text-displayed claims were not audited as behavioral commitments. Source: coordination/specs/Q-R71-SPEC.md:990 (AC-R71-9) + :992 (AC-R71-11) + :1049-1051 (discriminating-assertion table). Severity MINOR per REVIEWER-REPORT-R71.md MINOR-1. | R71 | ARCHITECT

Severity MINOR per REVIEWER-REPORT-R71.md MINOR-2. Not bound by any AC; tactical. | R71 | IMPLEMENTER

VIOLATION: dashboard-threshold-hardcoded-literal | tools/build-canned-demos.ts:1052 hardcodes `var LOG10_THRESHOLD = Math.log10(200)` as a literal in the dashboard JS, but the scenario JSONs serialize `params.threshold = 1/DEMO_ALPHA` (currently 200). If a future round changes DEMO_ALPHA, the dashboard chart threshold line silently drifts from the per-scenario encoded threshold. Should read scenario.params.threshold and recompute log10 per scenario. Severity MINOR per REVIEWER-REPORT-R71.md MINOR-3. | R71 | IMPLEMENTER

VIOLATION: per-shard-fired-flag-inline-literal-predicate | tools/build-canned-demos.ts:350 (common-mode-rack), :713 (sparse-data), :832 (topology-spanning) set per_shard.fired via inline literal predicate (`id === 'shard-00' || ...`) rather than deriving from the firedEvents list. A typo in the shard ID literal would silently mis-label which shards fired without breaking any AC. Should derive: `firedEvents.map(e => e.shard_node_id).includes(id)`. Severity MINOR per REVIEWER-REPORT-R71.md MINOR-4. | R71 | IMPLEMENTER

VIOLATION: in-spec-arithmetic | Spec § 2.2 example JSON comment + § 3.1 pseudocode comment + tools/coverage-saturation.ts:67 all claim "0x71C00 = 466016 decimal". Actual decimal value of 0x71C00 is 7*65536 + 1*4096 + 12*256 = 458752 + 4096 + 3072 = 465920. 466016 would be 0x71C60 (off by 96 / 0x60). Runtime matrix.json:3 correctly serializes the actual runtime value (465920); only the documentation comment is wrong. CLAUDE-ARCHITECT REINFORCED R05/R06 (in-spec arithmetic and hard-coded list count consistency) should have caught this at spec emit. Implementer propagated the error verbatim into source comment. [tessera] VIOLATION: ac-r84-14-structurally-flaky-race-condition | R84 REVIEWER MINOR-2: AC-R84-14 (worker.terminate() halts streaming) is structurally flaky in Node worker_threads context. The worker can post all 51 messages synchronously into the message queue before the main thread processes message #1 and calls terminate(). Implementer disclosed TD-3 (1 flake in ~5 full-suite runs; count=51). Reviewer 5× isolation reruns all PASS, but the race is real and structural (worker_threads.terminate() is async; synchronous message emission can outrun it). The discriminating property "fewer than 50 messages" is satisfied probabilistically, not by terminate() actually pre-empting emission. Discipline: end-to-end-test-race-conditions. | R84 | ARCHITECT
